NTNU · QUASI-2D · M13

NWAR · trends, forces and waves

2026-11-09T22:05:51.790678+01:00

Original C01: 309,441 cells · expanded W04 mesh: 572,176 cells. U = 18 m/s · Δt = 0.4 ms · 10 inner iterations · ρ = 1000 / 1.2 kg/m³.

Each campaign uses one recorded C01 reference. Preserve the physics and M13 cell-size controls, then record five measured wave cycles and verify repeatability of cycles 2–5; cycle 1 is excluded. The reference acceptance receipt records the PRE stability and compatibility policy. Legacy export labels CL, CD, L and D refer to fixed-axis Cz, Cx, Fz and Fx in CFD results. The polar lift and drag used by the quasi-steady model are defined relative to its incident flow.

Q data · CSV · Current results · CSV · MATLAB data · MATLAB plots · Audit and provenance

Q · horizontal force difference relative to C01

C01 calm-water reference: 27.00 N.

Q = ⟨Cx wave⟩ − ⟨Cx C01⟩. ΔFx = Q × 3855.6 N. Negative values indicate a reduction in horizontal force. Fx and Fz are measured in the fixed domain axes. Cx = Fx / 3855.6 N and Cz = Fz / 3855.6 N, with buoyancy subtracted from the vertical force. Each accepted wave contributes the mean of its complete measured cycles 2–5, excluding cycle 1.

Diagnostic means and provenance · JSON

Quasi-steady model comparison

The model uses the NACA66 XFOIL polar at Re = 20 million, 18 m/s current and 2 m foil depth. Calm lift sets the trim angle. The two wave phases come from the foil probe. The model projects the polar lift and drag onto the fixed domain axes before comparison. Both predictions use the same three-beat time window as CFD.

Force–wave comparison relative to C01

Means of cycles 2–5 at x = 0; cycle 1 excluded. The force curves show ΔFz and ΔFx in the fixed domain axes, with C01 as zero. Wave-cycle mean offsets are retained. Colours and line styles identify wave conditions consistently across all four panels.

PDF · vector · SVG · editable vector · PNG · 600 dpi · Curve data · CSV · Figure caption

Figure caption and phase reconstruction

Phase-averaged foil response relative to accepted C01 (W01, W02, W03, W04, W04c, W04e, W04f, W04g, W04h, W04i, W04j, W05, W06, W07). Panels (a,c) show vertical and horizontal force differences in fixed domain axes from C01 versus wave phase at x = 0; panels (b,d) show the corresponding force–surface-elevation loops. Each curve is the mean of the complete response cycles 2–5, excluding cycle 1, after subtracting the same fixed C01 force from every sample. Horizontal zero lines denote C01. Wave-cycle mean force offsets are retained. C01 buoyancy-corrected vertical force is 1217.0568 N and horizontal force is 26.9981 N. Force differences are in newtons; surface elevations are measured from each cycle mean in metres, with no amplitude normalization. Phase zero is the upward crossing of the wave reference level at x = 0. The grey dashed sin(phi) curves are dimensionless phase guides; they do not represent one physical wave height shared by all cases. Loop coordinates use each case’s wave signal. Circles and squares mark the measured-wave crest and trough; arrows indicate increasing time. Where the direct x = 0 history is incomplete, the wave signal is propagated from upstream probes. Travel time is calibrated from a probe pair where available and otherwise estimated from wavelength and period. Forces are not shifted or fitted to the wave. These results describe trends in the selected quasi-2D M13 configuration.

Stage criteria and future work
  1. This stage assesses trends and qualitative foil response. Acceptance requires the recorded C01 reference, a verified source, preserved physical settings, five complete cycles with repeatable cycles 2–5 and a healthy saved result.
  2. The M10–M13 review was completed before C01. Expanded domains preserve the M13 cell-size controls and foil layers. PRE W04 must pass two consecutive 0.5% stability checks. The accepted reference policy is recorded with every run.
  3. Q = mean wave Cx over cycles 2–5 (cycle 1 excluded) minus accepted C01 Cx. Negative Q indicates lower horizontal force. ΔFx is also reported in newtons.
  4. Force–wave plots use one common foil reference at x = 0 m for existing and future runs. Older signals are extrapolated from the recorded upstream probe using measured wave propagation, with reconstruction details saved in the evidence. A direct measurement at x = 0 is preferred when available. Force timing is unchanged; lag calibration uses wave probes only.
  5. Forcing and reflection quantification, time-step sensitivity, formal uncertainty and mass-flux closure are future studies; they do not block current acceptance or Q.

One current saved result per wave. W03 combines the original history and its continuation in a single result. Source files and previous reports remain preserved.

PRE W04 · calm preparation on the expanded mesh

Calm gate passed. Original C01 remains the reference for all Q values. PRE W04 is not a wave result and contributes no Q.

CL/CD stability below 0.5% in adjacent 0.2 s windows; both means within 0.5% of original C01; two consecutive eligible checks.

Fresh whole-domain startup: flat water from rest; synchronized 0–18 m/s ramp over 1 s at the inlet and throughout the fluid region. The temporary momentum source is removed after the ramp. This PRE history starts at zero; the previous attempt remains archived.

Automation verification

PASSED: actual solver start from C01, pause/save and continuous resume; 10 + 20 steps. C01 unchanged. This technical test is excluded from campaign results.

Test evidence · Measured-cycle counter replay

C01 reference and mesh review

Method and scope

Standard CL/CD histories show the current coefficients, with C01 drawn as a reference line. CL is hydrodynamic: the constant buoyancy contribution of 22.718189111947265 N is removed before division by 3855.6 N. C01 uses the same convention. Force-difference plots use L − L_C01 and D − D_C01; their zero lines denote C01. Wave-cycle mean force offsets remain visible.

Continuation histories are joined at verified time boundaries without changing the source CSV files. Dashed vertical lines mark every resume point in full-history plots and MATLAB exports.

All existing and future force–wave plots share the foil reference x = 0 m. A recorded measurement at this position is used directly. Otherwise the closest recorded upstream reference signal is extrapolated by its measured travel time to x = 0. W01 uses x = -6 m; W02 and W03 use x = -0.5 m. Propagation speed is measured from two upstream wave probes over their common settled history. The measured waveform and amplitude are retained. The reconstruction method and travel time are recorded in the evidence; local wave distortion is not reconstructed. If paired data are unavailable, a wavelength/period estimate is identified explicitly. Original raw samples are preserved.

The time shift and calibration evidence are saved in each force-wave JSON and CSV/MAT exports. Force data never determine the wave shift, and force timing is unchanged: the physical phase lead or lag remains visible. Each plot shows complete phase cycles 2–5 (cycle 1 excluded) with sufficient force data, with partial results labelled explicitly. Phase zero is the upward crossing of the wave reference level at x = 0 m. The acceptance counter and Q retain their independently recorded probe and averaging windows. Loop arrows indicate time direction.

New runs also record a direct probe at x = 0; the surface calculation accounts for the submerged foil section. Q describes trends relative to the accepted campaign C01. Cycle spread is not a formal uncertainty estimate. Partial runs and technical tests do not contribute to Q.
